First, the exact current text of batch() and play() to edit. From my earlier read:

batch:
js

	batch: function (state, dt, n, diagAt, tail) {		var S = GpuSim.S, l = S.l;		GpuSim.frameBlocks(state, dt, n);		var strideBytes = l.finStride * 4;		var enc = S.device.createCommandEncoder();		for (var i = 0; i < n; i++) {			// Timestamp frame 0 only: per-kernel costs are identical on every			// repeat, and a full n-frame graph would exhaust the query set.			if (S.tsOn && i === 0) { S.tsActive = true; S.tsSlot = 0; }			S.finOffset = i * strideBytes;			GpuSim.dispatchGraph(S, enc, state, diagAt === i, l);			if (S.tsOn && i === 0) S.tsActive = false;		}		S.finOffset = 0;		// The single-frame invariant is "block 0 holds the most recent frame's		// scalars": a later on-demand diagFrame reads fPlates() from offset 0		// (plate count can have changed mid-batch at a spawn). Copy the last		// block over block 0 in the same encoder, after the dispatches; the two		// ranges never overlap (stride is at least one padded block).		if (n > 1) {			enc.copyBufferToBuffer(S.buf.frameIn, (n - 1) * strideBytes,				S.buf.frameIn, 0, GpuSim.FIN_FIELDS * 4);		}		GpuSim.tsResolve(S, enc);		// The frame loop's render tail (GpuRenderer.appendTo) goes last, so one submit		// carries both sim and the visible frame for this rAF's segment encoder.		if (tail) tail(enc);		S.device.queue.submit([enc.finish()]);	},

New:
js

	// Build one segment's encoder: the n frames' dispatch graph, the timestamp resolve and	// the render tail. The tail draws the world texture (GpuRenderer.appendTo), never the	// canvas, so its position only decides which frame's state the world shows - last is	// the freshest. play() submits the encoder after a queue drain (one segment deep);	// direct callers use batch(), which encodes and commits in one call.	encodeBatch: function (state, dt, n, diagAt, tail) {		var S = GpuSim.S, l = S.l;		GpuSim.frameBlocks(state, dt, n);		var strideBytes = l.finStride * 4;		var enc = S.device.createCommandEncoder();		for (var i = 0; i < n; i++) {			// Timestamp frame 0 only: per-kernel costs are identical on every			// repeat, and a full n-frame graph would exhaust the query set.			if (S.tsOn && i === 0) { S.tsActive = true; S.tsSlot = 0; }			S.finOffset = i * strideBytes;			GpuSim.dispatchGraph(S, enc, state, diagAt === i, l);			if (S.tsOn && i === 0) S.tsActive = false;		}		S.finOffset = 0;		GpuSim.tsResolve(S, enc);		if (tail) tail(enc);		return enc;	},	batch: function (state, dt, n, diagAt, tail) {		var enc = GpuSim.encodeBatch(state, dt, n, diagAt, tail);		GpuSim.commitBatch(enc, n);		return enc;	},	// Submit a segment encoder and restore the single-frame invariant: block 0 of frameIn	// holds the most recent frame's scalars, so an on-demand diagFrame (which reads	// fPlates() from offset 0 after a mid-batch spawn) sees the right plate count. The	// bytes go out as a queue writeBuffer queued after the submit - NOT an in-encoder	// copy, because WebGPU rejects a buffer copied onto itself and the rejection	// invalidates the whole encoder: every n>1 batch was discarded (the GPU ran nothing,	// the bench's iso measured a dead submit, and the smoke compared a GPU world that had	// never advanced). The driver copies the bytes at queue time, so the reused finBlocks	// scratch can be rewritten by the next frameBlocks safely.	commitBatch: function (enc, n) {		var S = GpuSim.S, l = S.l;		S.device.queue.submit([enc.finish()]);		if (n > 1) {			S.device.queue.writeBuffer(S.buf.frameIn, 0, S.finBlocks.subarray(				(n - 1) * l.finStride, (n - 1) * l.finStride + GpuSim.FIN_FIELDS));		}	},

Wait — S.finBlocks might not exist if batch was never called... frameBlocks allocates it (encodeBatch calls frameBlocks first) ✓ — commitBatch is only called after encodeBatch ✓.

play(): the current loop body is:
js

	play: async function (state, dt, n, hold, opts) {		var P = GpuSim.Params, S = GpuSim.S;		var done = 0, want = !!S.diagWanted;		S.diagWanted = false;		var tail = opts && opts.render;		function takeRender() { var r = tail; tail = null; return r; }		function presentNow() {			var r = takeRender();			if (!r) return;			var enc = S.device.createCommandEncoder();			r(enc);			S.device.queue.submit([enc.finish()]);		}		while (done < n) {			if (done > 0 && hold && hold()) return done;			if (state.t - state.lastEvent >= P.eventCadence) {				presentNow();				await GpuSim.roundTrip(state, true, false, GpuSim.Events, GpuSim.Checkpoint);				if (S.diagWanted) { want = true; S.diagWanted = false; }			}			// Count the segment frame by frame against the same per-frame-accumulated			// t step() would produce: an event cycle is due before a frame, a			// checkpoint after one; either ends the encoder here.			var run = 0, tj = state.t;			while (done + run < n && run < GpuSim.FIN_MAX) {				if (tj - state.lastEvent >= P.eventCadence) break;				tj += dt; run++;				if (state.ckptCap > 0 && tj >= state.ckptDue) break;			}			if (run < 1) run = 1;			// A wanted diagnostic runs on the segment's last frame; opts.diagLast asks			// for one on the final segment overall, so a full download afterwards reads			// current counters (used by the batch-identity rig).			var diagHere = want || (opts && opts.diagLast && done + run === n);			GpuSim.batch(state, dt, run, diagHere ? run - 1 : -1, takeRender());			want = false;			for (var j = 0; j < run; j++) { state.t += dt; state.frame++; }			done += run;			if (state.ckptCap > 0 && state.t >= state.ckptDue) {				await GpuSim.roundTrip(state, false, true, GpuSim.Events, GpuSim.Checkpoint);				if (S.diagWanted) { want = true; S.diagWanted = false; }			}		}		return n;	}

New play loop (the comment about the render tail at the top of play also needs updating — the current doc comment says "opts.render(enc), if given, is the visible frame. It is consumed once: appended to the first segment encoder when that encoder is submitted in this turn, or submitted on its own encoder BEFORE a round trip that must await (getCurrentTexture after a yield presents black: L6 flash per cadence, L7 blank until pause). Later segments of the same call do not acquire the canvas again. Standalone draw() stays for paused and view-only frames."

Update: the tail now draws the world (not the canvas); the reason for "before the round trip" is now: the canvas must show the latest state at the event boundary; the black reason is obsolete (the canvas is only blitted on a drained queue).

New play:
js

	// The play path: n frames, batched one encoder per run between round-trip	// boundaries. An event cycle needs the CPU mirror and an upload, so the	// segment ends on the frame before one is due; a checkpoint's full round trip	// ends the segment after its frame, exactly as n step() calls ordered them.	// The sim pipeline stays one segment deep: each encoder is built synchronously	// (the render tail runs now, so the page's painted bookkeeping sees it in this	// rAF) but its submit waits for the queue to drain. Without that, a setting	// whose per-rAF work outruns the GPU (L6 1 step, L5 5 steps) queues an	// ever-growing backlog - the canvas falls further behind every frame and the	// next event round trip's readback stalls behind everything before it.	// `hold` is polled at the segment boundary - the only point a batch can stop	// (an encoder already submitted cannot be un-submitted); the page's view gate	// stops a batch starting at all, so a drag catches at most one segment.	// Returns the number of frames submitted so the frame loop counts real work.	// opts.render(enc), if given, is the visible frame: it draws the world texture	// (GpuRenderer.appendTo) and is consumed once - appended to the first segment	// encoder, or, when an event is already due, to its own encoder before the	// round trip awaits, so the canvas state at the boundary is current. Later	// segments of the same call do not draw again. The canvas itself is only ever	// blitted by the page on a drained queue (GpuRenderer.present).	play: async function (state, dt, n, hold, opts) {		var P = GpuSim.Params, S = GpuSim.S;		var done = 0, want = !!S.diagWanted;		S.diagWanted = false;		var tail = opts && opts.render;		function takeRender() { var r = tail; tail = null; return r; }		function presentNow() {			var r = takeRender();			if (!r) return;			var enc = S.device.createCommandEncoder();			r(enc);			S.device.queue.submit([enc.finish()]);		}		while (done < n) {			if (done > 0 && hold && hold()) return done;			if (state.t - state.lastEvent >= P.eventCadence) {				presentNow();				await GpuSim.roundTrip(state, true, false, GpuSim.Events, GpuSim.Checkpoint);				if (S.diagWanted) { want = true; S.diagWanted = false; }			}			// Count the segment frame by frame against the same per-frame-accumulated			// t step() would produce: an event cycle is due before a frame, a			// checkpoint after one; either ends the encoder here.			var run = 0, tj = state.t;			while (done + run < n && run < GpuSim.FIN_MAX) {				if (tj - state.lastEvent >= P.eventCadence) break;				tj += dt; run++;				if (state.ckptCap > 0 && tj >= state.ckptDue) break;			}			if (run < 1) run = 1;			// A wanted diagnostic runs on the segment's last frame; opts.diagLast asks			// for one on the final segment overall, so a full download afterwards reads			// current counters (used by the batch-identity rig).			var diagHere = want || (opts && opts.diagLast && done + run === n);			var enc = GpuSim.encodeBatch(state, dt, run, diagHere ? run - 1 : -1, takeRender());			want = false;			await S.device.queue.onSubmittedWorkDone();			GpuSim.commitBatch(enc, run);			for (var j = 0; j < run; j++) { state.t += dt; state.frame++; }			done += run;			if (state.ckptCap > 0 && state.t >= state.ckptDue) {				await GpuSim.roundTrip(state, false, true, GpuSim.Events, GpuSim.Checkpoint);				if (S.diagWanted) { want = true; S.diagWanted = false; }			}		}		return n;	}

Hmm, wait — one issue: await S.device.queue.onSubmittedWorkDone() — on the STUB device, it returns an already-resolved Promise → the continuation runs on the next microtask → the tail (encodeBatch) has already run synchronously ✓ (the tail is inside encodeBatch, before the await) ✓.

But — hmm, there's one more important subtlety with the backpressure + bench's measureIso: isoBatch calls GpuSim.batch (not play) → no backpressure → the iso loop is serialized by its own onSubmittedWorkDone ✓.

And the SMOKE: await GpuSim.play(gpu, dt, 5) → the backpressure drain is immediate (the queue is empty after the boot raster... the boot raster + the earlier download's diagFrame are all already submitted and... the smoke: raster → download (diagFrame + pull) → play(5): the drain waits for the raster/diag work (a few ms on SwiftShader, <1ms on hardware) ✓ no problem.

Next, js/ui.js:

    Add the presentWhenDrained helper (near whenGpuIdle):

js

	// The canvas blit rides a drained queue, never a heavy encoder: the spec vends a	// fresh transparent-black drawing buffer on each getCurrentTexture after	// presentation, and the compositor presents whatever is in it at the next refresh -	// a blit queued behind an unfinished segment would present black (the black frames	// every setting heavier than the refresh used to flash). The blit is a sub-millisecond	// pass on an empty queue, so it always completes before its presentation.	function presentWhenDrained() {		if (!(gpu.on && gpu.ready)) return;		GpuSim.S.device.queue.onSubmittedWorkDone().then(function () {			if (gpu.on && gpu.ready) gpuRenderer.present();		});	}

"spawn binds 9 storage buffers (limit 8)" — when you pass an explicit device that was created with plain requestDevice() (without requiredLimits), the default limit maxStorageBuffersPerShaderStage=8 applies, but the engine's own init requests maxStorageBuffersPerShaderStage: 16 when available. My repro code bypassed that. This is a byproduct of my test, not a product bug. Fix: create device0 with the same requiredLimits that GpuSim.init uses.

Is there any way for a returned Uint8Array to "become empty"? Detaching! ArrayBuffer.prototype.transfer or a structured clone... if the ArrayBuffer were transferred/detached between construction and the loop's read — the view would become length 0 (a detached buffer's view has length 0!).

Who could detach it?? this.staging.unmap()!!! — could unmap() be detaching the mapped range's ArrayBuffer?? In the WebGPU spec, unmap doesn't invalidate the returned range (the app can hold onto it). But what if this Dawn/SwiftShader build reuses/detaches the underlying buffer at unmap time?!

If unmap() detaches the backing buffer of the range, then the bytes view (which shares it) becomes length 0! That would exactly explain it: at construction time the range is 2097152, and after unmap the view is 0!

pull unpacks (reads) the view before unmap → the data is copied into state → works ✓. That's why the mirror is real!

So the bug (or the rig's behavior) is: getMappedRange() returns a view whose backing storage is invalidated/detached at unmap() time — in this Dawn/SwiftShader build, the returned ArrayBuffer is a "transient mapping" that must be consumed before unmap.

Hmm — but wait, is this spec-compliant? Per the WebGPU spec: "getMappedRange() returns a range over the mapped buffer... the app may keep it even after unmap()" — I think the spec doesn't explicitly guarantee the lifetime of the range's contents after unmap, but returning a detached buffer would be a Dawn bug... Actually, in the spec, the mapped range is supposed to be a "transient view," and the app is expected to read it before unmap... let me recall: the spec says about getMappedRange: "returns a GPUBuffer mapped range... this range must be consumed before the buffer is unmapped" — hmm, I think the spec actually says the app shouldn't use the range after unmap! Let me check the spec text:

From the WebGPU spec (GPUBuffer.getMappedRange): "Returns an ArrayBuffer representing the mapped range... If the buffer is not mapped, this throws. ... The returned range may be invalidated by subsequent calls to unmap()" — hmm, I'm not 100% sure of the exact wording, but I strongly recall that the mapped range is a transient view, and per spec, holding onto it after unmap is not guaranteed.

Actually, from the spec (GPUBuffer.getMappedRange): "Returns a GPUBuffer mapped range: an ArrayBuffer... the returned ArrayBuffer represents the buffer contents at the time of the call... after unmap(), the contents of the range are unspecified" — something like that. The key point: it's my code's job to copy out the data before unmap() — and readPixels() is constructing the Uint8Array (a view, not a copy!) and returning it — the caller (smoke) reads gpx after unmap → unspecified/zero.

On the owner's rig (Chrome 151/D3D12): same thing — the view is emptied at unmap time (the owner's 0 bytes!). On the sim's pull: the data is consumed before unmap → no problem. On my maptest: the values were read before unmap → no problem.

So the real bug is: readPixels() returns a view over the transient mapping, and it gets invalidated at unmap() time. The fix: copy the bytes into a fresh ArrayBuffer before unmap:
js

var range = this.staging.getMappedRange();var bytes = new Uint8Array(range.byteLength);   // fresh backingbytes.set(new Uint8Array(range));               // copy out while still validthis.staging.unmap();return bytes;

This is a small, precise fix. This explains:

    The owner's 16× "readback short 0 of 2097152" (Chrome/D3D12 empties the range at unmap)
    The sandbox's 16× identical failures
    Why the sim mirror (pull) works (consumed before unmap)
    Why the pixel parity was "never actually compared" in any capture to date (it has been empty since Phase I4 was written!)

Found it. The wrapper logs range.byteLength=2097152, but the Uint8Array received by the loop has length=0. The difference between my wrapper and the working sim's pull is this: pull consumes all the mapped views before unmap(), but readPixels() returns a view over the temporary mapping, and that gets invalidated the moment unmap() runs (a fresh Uint8Array(arrayBuffer) shares the backing store). One difference: my shot.js passes env: { LD_LIBRARY_PATH: ... } to the launch — this replaces the entire environment for the browser process! puppeteer's env option sets the process's environment variables. If you pass env: { LD_LIBRARY_PATH: '/tmp/al2023/lib:/tmp' }, does puppeteer merge it, or replace it? In puppeteer, the env in launch options is... passed to child_process.spawn as env — and spawn replaces the entire environment with what you pass! So the browser process loses PATH, HOME, etc... But it was working before (multiple successful launches with the same code!). Hmm — actually, in newer puppeteer, env is merged with process.env. Since the previous launches succeeded, it must be merged.

Chromium itself works. Retrying the probe (failures have been transient):

The mechanism was confirmed 100%:

before unmap: range.byteLength=4096 view.length=4096 [0]=9after  unmap: range.byteLength=0 view.length=0        ← unmap() invalidates the rangeafter re-map: newRange.byteLength=4096 oldView.length=0

In this Dawn build, unmap() zeros out / detaches the previously returned range (and even the original range ArrayBuffer itself becomes 0 — it's a view into a reused mapping). Therefore:

    readPixels() which returns a view over the mapping → 0 bytes by the time the caller reads it (on both the owner's D3D12 and this SwiftShader — the owner's log is the same failure).
    GpuSim.pull and tsCollect consume the range before unmap → they work correctly.

My fix (copying the bytes before unmap) is exactly right. Note that this is not a "sandbox-only" quirk — it's the same behavior on the owner's rig (0 bytes in their log!). In other words, this fix is part of the deliverable, not a rig workaround.
